--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (N and G), we need to analyze the paths between these nodes and find the critical nodes whose removal will break all paths.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify direct connections to the black nodes:**
   - Node N is directly connected to nodes B and C.
   - Node G is directly connected to nodes B, T, and H.

2. **Identify paths from N to G:**
   - Path 1: N → B → G
   - Path 2: N → B → H → G
   - Path 3: N → B → F → T → G
   - Path 4: N → B → C → H → G
   - Path 5: N → C → H → G
   - Path 6: N → C → H → I → Q → R → J → E → F → T → G

3. **Analyze the critical nodes:**
   - To disconnect N and G, we need to remove nodes that are common in all paths or whose removal will break multiple paths.
   - Node B is a critical node in Path 1, Path 2, Path 3, and Path 4.
   - Node H is a critical node in Path 2, Path 4, and Path 5.
   - Node T is a critical node in Path 3.

4. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing node B will disconnect Path 1, Path 2, Path 3, and Path 4.
   - Removing node H will disconnect Path 2, Path 4, and Path 5.
   - Removing node T will disconnect Path 3 and Path 6.

   However, removing just B, H, and T is not sufficient to disconnect all paths. We need to consider additional nodes to ensure complete disconnection.

5. **Find the minimum set of nodes to remove:**
   - Removing nodes B, H, and T disconnects most paths, but we need to ensure that Path 6 is also disconnected.
   - Adding node F to the cut set will break Path 3 and Path 6.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes is 4.

### Answer:
Nodes to remove: 4, Cut Set: [B, H, T, F]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['B', 'C']}

   - Removing the edges (H, B), (Q, J), and (Q, P) will also disconnect all paths between H and Q.

After evaluating the paths and the critical edges, we find that the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes H and Q is 3.

   - Node B is connected to A, E, S, T, G, and H.

   - Path 2: A → B → H

   - **I** is directly connected to **B**, **E**, **P**, **S**, **T**, **J**.

2. **Connections of Neighboring Nodes**:
   - **P**: Connected to **A**, **B**, **E**, **Q**, **I**.
   - **B**: Connected to **A**, **E**, **P**, **Q**, **I**.
   - **T**: Connected to **E**, **S**, **F**, **C**, **Q**, **I**.
   - **J**: Connected to **F**, **G**, **H**, **I**, **Q**.

   - **S**: Connected to **N**, **E**, **F**, **T**, **I**.

   - **Q -> P -> I**
   - **Q -> B -> I**
   - **Q -> T -> I**
   - **Q -> J -> I**
   - **Q -> T -> S -> E -> I**

4. **Bottleneck Evaluation**:
   - **Q -> P -> I**: Remove (P, I) or (P, Q).
   - **Q -> B -> I**: Remove (B, I) or (B, Q).
   - **Q -> T -> I**: Remove (T, I) or (T, Q).
   - **Q -> J -> I**: Remove (J, I) or (J, Q).
   - **Q -> T -> S -> E -> I**: Remove (T, S), (S, E), or (E, I).

5. **Minimal Cut Set**:
   - By removing edges **(P, I)**, **(B, I)**, and **(T, I)**, we effectively disconnect **Q** from **I** across all possible paths.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to be removed is 3.

     - \( N \rightarrow G \rightarrow B \rightarrow E \)

   - Remove \( G \rightarrow B \)
   - Remove \( N \rightarrow G \) or \( N \rightarrow R \rightarrow J \rightarrow G \) (but it is better to remove a direct path).

3. Ensure that the solution is minimal, meaning no fewer nodes can achieve the same disconnection.

### Step 1: Identify All Paths Between H and R

- **Path 1:** H -> I -> J -> O -> R
- **Path 2:** H -> I -> J -> G -> C -> N -> R
- **Path 3:** H -> I -> J -> G -> C -> B -> A -> S -> N -> R
- **Path 4:** H -> I -> J -> G -> C -> B -> E -> S -> N -> R
- **Path 5:** H -> I -> J -> G -> C -> B -> F -> T -> B -> E -> S -> N -> R
- **Path 6:** H -> I -> J -> G -> C -> B -> T -> F -> E -> S -> N -> R

- Removing **I** will disconnect Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6.
- Removing **J** will disconnect Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6.
- Removing **G** will disconnect Paths 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6.
- Removing **O** will disconnect Path 1.

From the analysis, we see that removing **I** and **J** will disconnect all the paths between H and R. Removing either **I** or **J** alone will not be sufficient as there are alternate paths through the other node.

### Step 3: Ensure Minimality

We need to verify that removing any other combination of nodes will not achieve the same disconnection with fewer nodes. From the analysis, removing **I** and **J** is the minimal set that disconnects all paths.

### Conclusion

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph such that the two black nodes are completely disconnected is:

   - Path 1: E -> B -> H -> N
   - Path 2: E -> B -> J -> H -> N

   - Path 4: E -> B -> S -> T -> I -> N

   - Path 7: E -> F -> T -> N
   - Path 8: E -> F -> T -> I -> N
   - Path 9: E -> F -> C -> S -> T -> I -> N
7. **Determine the minimum cut set**: To disconnect E and N, we need to remove the critical edges that connect these paths. Upon closer inspection, removing the edges (E, B), (B, S), and (T, N) will disconnect all possible paths between E and N.
